== Introduction

This document describes the construction procedures to be performed before operating the DCI-2.0 system, targeting the tasks to be carried out by DCI operators (<<用語集,see Glossary>>). +
Please note that tasks performed by tenant operators are not covered in this document.

By completing the procedures in this document, the DCI management cluster (DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure) will be operating normally, and tenant logical infrastructure provisioning will be available. Specifically,

* Completing Chapters 3 and 4 will finish the initial construction.
* For server expansion, only perform Chapter 4.
* For operational tasks after construction, refer to the Operation Manual.

Please note that reading this document requires basic knowledge of Linux and Kubernetes.

=== Terminology

The terminology used in this document is shown below.

.Glossary
[[用語集]]
[cols="1,1,4", options="header"]
|===
|Category |Term |Description

|DCI-2.0 Proprietary Terms
|DCI Controller
|A general term for software that controls and manages the DCI system. It operates on each component: DCI Manager, DCI Physical Infrastructure, and DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure.

|
|DCI System
|The entire system including software, hardware, and network equipment of DCI Manager, DCI Physical Infrastructure, and DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure.

|
|DCI Manager
|Functions for managing DCI Physical Infrastructure and DCI Tenants. It handles tenant provisioning/deletion and logical infrastructure construction/management.

|
|DCI Tenant
|An environment for tenant operators configured on DCI Physical Infrastructure. It consists of DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure and DCI Apps.

|
|DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure
|Logically independent infrastructure for each tenant, constructed and managed by DCI Manager. It includes DCI App execution environments and common management functions.

|
|DCI App
|Applications constructed and operated by tenant operators. Composed of Kubernetes (K8s) Pods.

|
|App Image
|A general term for container images of user-specific apps and standard-provided apps.

|
|User-Specific App
|Applications registered and used by tenant operators.

|
|Standard-Provided App
|Applications registered by DCI operators and commonly used across multiple tenants.

|
|DCI Management Server
|Servers not included in physical resources, used for DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure software. (Servers not used as logical servers for DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure)

|
|DCI Management Cluster
|A Kubernetes cluster composed of DCI management server groups and VMs on servers, where DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure software commonly operate.

|
|DCI Provider
|The entity that introduces, installs, and operates the DCI system.

|
|DCI Operator
|One of the actors described in this document. Operates the entire DCI system as a department of the DCI Provider.

|
|Tenant Operator
|One of the actors described in this document. Uses the DCI Tenant to operate DCI Apps.

|Kubernetes
|Kubernetes
|Container orchestration software. Abbreviated as K8s.

|
|K8s Cluster
|A container execution environment managed by Kubernetes. Composed of Control Plane and Nodes.

|
|K8s Control Plane
|A group of components that manage the entire K8s cluster.

|
|K8s Node
|A Node that executes workloads in a K8s cluster.

|
|Node
|A logical server connected to a logical network, usable as a K8s Node.

|CDI
|CDI
|Composable Disaggregated Infrastructure. A platform that manages devices individually and flexibly combines them to build servers.

|
|Composed Server
|A logical server constructed by combining devices from the device pool using CDI.

|
|Compute Server
|A server in a CDI environment used for dynamically allocating and utilizing resources such as GPUs, NICs, and SSDs.

|
|PG-CDI
|Abbreviation for Primergy CDI. A CDI solution provided by Fujitsu Limited.

|Network
|Physical Network
|A general term for network equipment composed of L2 switches, optical-electrical convergence L2 switches, etc.

|
|Logical Network
|A network configured on physical networks that is logically independent between tenants.

|
|App Data Network
|A network for main signal and application data communication.

|
|CNI Network
|A network for Kubernetes CNI communication.

|
|Host Network
|A network for DCI Physical Infrastructure host communication.

|
|Manager Network
|A network for DCI Physical Infrastructure and DCI Manager management.

|
|Physical Network
|A component of DCI Physical Infrastructure; a general term for network equipment composed of L2 switches, optical-electrical convergence L2 switches, etc.

|
|Logical Network
|A network logically created on the physical network of DCI Physical Infrastructure, used in DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure. A network logically independent from other DCI Tenants.

|Infrastructure/Resources
|DCI Physical Infrastructure
|Hardware groups and their management functions that compose the DCI system.

|
|Physical Resources
|A general term for COTS servers, CDI managed devices, and their controllers.

|
|COTS Server
|A pre-assembled commercial off-the-shelf server.

|
|Device
|Server components such as CPU, memory, GPU, storage, and NIC.

|
|Resource Pool
|A collection of physical resources not yet assigned to a DCI Tenant.

|
|COTS Server Pool
|A collection of COTS servers not assigned to a DCI Tenant.

|
|Device Pool
|A collection of CDI managed devices not assigned to a DCI Tenant.

|
|Logical Server
|A general term for COTS servers, composed servers, and VMs.

|
|Configuration Management DB
|A database that centrally manages information about all elements composing DCI, including physical infrastructure, logical infrastructure, and network settings.

|I/O Devices
|Card
|A general term for PCIe cards.

|
|RDMA
|Remote Direct Memory Access. A technology for direct memory-to-memory communication over a network.

|
|GPUDirect RDMA
|A technology for direct communication between GPU memories.

|
|SR-IOV
|A technology for virtualizing and assigning physical devices.

|
|VF
|A virtual device generated by SR-IOV.

|External Systems
|External System (DCI Operations, etc.)
|External systems such as OSS/BSS related to DCI operations.

|
|External System (Tenant Operations, etc.)
|External systems used for tenant operations and application development.

|
|External System (Cloud)
|Systems on the cloud that collaborate with DCI Apps.

|Other
|Components
|A general term for OSS and DCI-2.0 deliverables deployed in DCI Manager, DCI Physical Infrastructure, and DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure within this system.

|
|Developed Software
|Software developed based on requirements definitions.

=== Prerequisites

==== Hardware Installation Requirements

The hardware installation procedures for system setup are outside the scope of this document; only the requirements are described.

The following are requirements for hardware installation:

* (Requirement 1) Physical environments (COTS servers, CDI equipment, etc.) must be installed and initially configured based on <<ハードウェア構成,Hardware Configuration>> and <<ハードウェア推奨スペック,Recommended Hardware Specifications>>, and network equipment must be installed and initially configured based on <<ネットワーク構成,Network Configuration>>, so that the physical environment is connected to and can communicate over the various networks.
* (Requirement 2) COTS servers and CDI equipment must have IPMI ports configured, and L2 network equipment must have management ports configured, enabling remote operation.
* (Requirement 3) Networks must be separated into manager and tenant (host, CNI, app data) networks, and VLAN IDs and subnets for tenant use must be pre-defined for the number of tenants the system supports.
* (Requirement 4) The subnets (separated by VLAN IDs) used by tenant NICs (host, CNI) must have L3 routing configured between the manager network and tenant network via L3 switches or routers, for the number of subnets defined in Requirement 3.
* (Requirement 5) All ports on L2 switch equipment connected to tenant NICs (host, CNI, app data) must not have any VLAN IDs configured (including removal of default VLAN IDs).
==== Expected Prerequisites


The following external services and environments are assumed to be prepared in advance.

* An NFS server has been built and is mountable from the DCI management cluster and DCI tenant infrastructure
* A DNS server is available and can resolve names within the manager network
* An NTP server is available and can synchronize time across all servers
* MinIO has been built as backup storage (for DCI management cluster and DCI tenant infrastructure)


==== How to Verify Prerequisites

Before starting construction, verify that the following requirements are met.
Verification should be performed through design documents or management tables, and by conducting connectivity checks on actual equipment.

* (Requirement 1) Ping from the management network to the management IP of each COTS server must succeed
* (Requirement 2) Login to IPMI/management ports via Web or CLI must be possible
* (Requirement 3) A list of tenant VLAN IDs and subnets must be organized in design documents or management tables
* (Requirement 4) Routes to the subnets defined in Requirement 3 must be configured on L3 switches or routers
* (Requirement 5) No VLANs must be configured on any ports of L2 switches for host network, CNI network, and app data network

=== Documentation Policy and Procedure Description Rules

==== Procedure Documentation Policy

The procedures described in this document are based on the fundamental policy that
even a person performing the construction work for the first time can reproduce the work
by following the documented content.
Each procedure clearly states the purpose of the task, execution procedure, and how to verify the execution results,
so that rollback procedures or restart points are identifiable in case of failure.
Execution procedures include the actual commands and operations to be entered without omission,
and expected output examples or screen display examples are provided as needed.

Variable elements such as IP addresses and version numbers are managed in a separate parameter sheet as a rule,
and the procedures include representative values along with corresponding variable names.
Representative values are intended to help understand the input format and value examples;
please replace them with the values defined in the parameter sheet during actual construction.
This approach minimizes the impact of modifications when parameters change
and provides documentation that considers future procedure automation.

==== Procedure Description Rules

Each work procedure described in this document is, as a rule, documented based on the following structure.

* Purpose of the task
* Execution procedure
  ** Command example
  ** Execution result example
* Verification procedure after execution
  ** Verification command example
  ** Expected verification result
* Rollback procedure in case of failure

However, for tasks such as incident response or tasks performed only when necessary, it may not be possible to explicitly state all items due to their nature.

Also, depending on the task content, verification procedures may be documented collectively after multiple execution procedures.
Rollback procedures may also be documented collectively for multiple procedures.

==== Notes on Environment and Version Differences

The screen captures, command examples, execution results, and verification results described in this document are examples for explanatory purposes.
Depending on the environment, software version, configuration state, etc., the displayed content or wording may partially differ, but as long as there is no significant deviation from the documented content, it does not affect the feasibility of the procedure or the correctness of the results.

The software version numbers and various parameter values described in this document are also examples, and the actual values to be used are managed in a separate parameter sheet.

==== Notation Rules

The following notations are used in subsequent chapters.

* `@k8s Node`:
  Indicates tasks to be performed on **the K8s Nodes of the "DCI management cluster"**.
* `@control plane`:
  Indicates tasks to be performed on **the K8s control plane of the "DCI management cluster"**.
  Even when adopting a redundant configuration for the DCI management cluster control plane, it is sufficient to perform the task on a single control plane Node.

== System Construction Overview

This chapter describes the overall configuration of the DCI-2.0 system targeted for construction in this manual and the positioning of the construction work.
The configuration and network configuration shown in this chapter primarily focus on the "DCI management cluster" constructed in Chapter 3.
Chapter 4, on the other hand, covers the initial configuration of physical resources (COTS servers, etc.) used for DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure construction.
Please understand the overall picture in this chapter by following the construction flow shown in Figure 5, then proceed with the procedures in Chapters 3 and 4 in order.

=== Network Configuration
The following shows an example of the network configuration that is a prerequisite for this manual's procedures. +
The network configuration covered in this document is a minimum configuration example for constructing the DCI management cluster and DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure.
For tenant networks (host, CNI, app data), it is assumed that VLAN IDs and subnets for the number of tenants have been designed and secured in advance.
Please design the IP address ranges and VLAN IDs for each network according to the environment and capacity being used.
Please pay careful attention during IP address design to avoid overlapping with subnets used internally by Kubernetes and CRI-O.


.Network Configuration
image::media/media/image01.png[width=1000]

=== Configuration Used in This Manual's Construction Procedures
This manual uses a minimum configuration as an example, showing the physical server configuration within the construction scope and the representative DCI-2.0 components deployed on them. The basic purpose is to install and configure the components listed below, bringing the DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure to an operational state as a DCI-2.0 system, and to provide the procedures for doing so. +
If constructing the DCI management cluster in a virtual machine (VM) environment, please refer to <<仮想マシン(VM)環境でのDCI管理用クラスタ構築手順,DCI Management Cluster Construction Procedure in Virtual Machine (VM) Environment>> in the Appendix.

=== Construction Flow

The construction work performed in this manual is broadly divided into the following two procedures.

* Procedure to construct the DCI management cluster and deploy DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure
* Procedure to perform initial configuration of various resources used in DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure

The overall picture of the construction work is shown in the figure below. +
By performing these tasks from top to bottom, the DCI environment can be constructed step by step. +
The numbers in the figure correspond to each chapter number. +
In the subsequent chapters, the specific work procedures are explained in order for each work block shown in this figure.

.Construction Flow
image::media/media/flow0.png[width=432]

== DCI Management Cluster Construction Procedure

=== Initial Construction

This section describes the initial construction procedures required before starting the DCI management cluster construction. +
Specifically, it describes the PG-CDI initial construction procedures.

This section shows the procedures corresponding to the area enclosed in the red frame in 2.4. Construction Flow.

image::media/media/flow1.png[width=432]

.Rollback Procedure
If a problem occurs in the procedures of each section in this chapter, as a general rule, redo the procedures of that section from the beginning. +
If a rollback method or restart point is explicitly stated within an individual procedure, follow those instructions.

==== PG-CDI Initial Construction

===== Setup

Refer to the following PG-CDI manual to set up PG-CDI.

 Reference: "PRIMERGY CDI V1.1 System Construction Procedure Manual"

Setup Overview
****
* Hardware Installation
* Hardware Setup
** Management Server Startup
** Client Linux-PC Startup
** Director Initial Configuration
** PCIe Switch Initial Configuration
* Software Setup
** Guest OS Initial Configuration and Startup
** CDI Management Software Configuration
* Certificate Configuration
****

===== Group Creation

Refer to the following PG-CDI manual to create resource group0. 

 Reference: "PRIMERGY CDI V1.1 System Operation Administrator Guide" Appendix A.1.3 Group Creation

===== Symbolic Link Configuration

On the client Linux-PC (ubuntu) set up during PG-CDI setup, create a symbolic link as the ubuntu user from the path where CDI commands were installed (/home/ubuntu/bin) to the CDI control path (/home/cdiadmin/bin). +
* If the /home/cdiadmin directory does not exist, please create it.

.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo mkdir -p /home/cdiadmin
$ sudo ln -s /home/ubuntu/bin /home/cdiadmin/bin
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo su
# cd ../cdiadmin
# ls -l
# exit
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
total 0
lrwxrwxrwx 1 root root 16 Apr  7 20:03 bin -> /home/ubuntu/bin
----

===== expect Module Installation

Install the expect module on the client Linux-PC as OSS required for PG-CDI control.

.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo apt install expect
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(omitted)
The following NEW packages will be installed:
  expect
(omitted)
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo apt list expect
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Listing... Done
expect/jammy,now 5.45.4-2build1 amd64 [installed]
----

This completes "PG-CDI Initial Construction".

==== OS Installation and Initial Configuration

This section describes the installation procedure assuming RedHat Enterprise Linux server 9.6 (RHEL9.6) as the OS to be installed. +
The target devices are all COTS servers of the DCI management servers in <<物理サーバ構成と代表的な構成品,Physical Server Configuration and Representative Components>>.

This procedure describes the installation method using the Integrated Dell Remote Access Controller (iDRAC) virtual console. +
* iDRAC is a dedicated management controller for remote management that comes standard on Dell servers.

The RHEL installation ISO image uses a DVD ISO image file.

.Prerequisites
* An iDRAC user account (username, password) must be registered in advance.
* You must have the DVD ISO image file for installation. Example: rhel-9.6-x86++_++64-dvd.iso (rhel-<RHEL Version>-x86_64-dvd.iso)

===== RHEL Installation

. Log in to iDRAC. +
+
--
Enter the username and password and click Submit.

image::media/media/image55.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Connect to iDRAC and launch the virtual console.
+
--
image::media/media/image3.png[width=1000]
--
. Click "Connect Virtual Media".
+
--
image::media/media/image4.png[width=1000]
--
. After selecting the installation target, click "Map Device".
+
--
Input example: In Map CD/DVD Image File, select "rhel-9.6-x86++_++64-dvd.iso" (rhel-<RHEL Version>-x86_64-dvd.iso)

image::media/media/image5.png[width=1000]
--
. In the virtual console, select "Console Controls -> Apply Ctrl-Alt-Del", restart the server, and select "F11 = Boot Manager" on the BIOS screen.
+
--
image::media/media/image6.png[width=1000]
image::media/media/image7.png[width=1000]
--
. After selecting "One-shot BIOS Boot Menu", select "Virtual Optical Drive".
+
--
image::media/media/image9.png[width=1000]
image::media/media/image8.png[width=1000]
--
. After waiting a few minutes, the boot process will begin.
+
--
image::media/media/image10.png[width=1000]
--
. On the welcome screen, select Japanese.
+
--
image::media/media/1.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Select the installation destination.
+
--
image::media/media/2.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Select the installation destination disk and click Done.
+
--
image::media/media/3.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Select "Software Selection".
+
--
image::media/media/4.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Select "Server" and click "Done".
+
--
image::media/media/5.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Select "Network & Host Name".
+
--
image::media/media/6.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Select the manager network interface and click "Configure".
+
--
image::media/media/7.jpg[width=1000]
--
. For network configuration, only minimal settings are configured as detailed configuration is difficult on the installation screen.
+
--
Specifically, only the manager network and DNS server are configured with the goal of "making the system reachable via SSH from external networks".

* Configuration example: Enter the following from IPv4 settings.
** IP Address: <MGR-IPAddr> Example shown: 172.18.161.23
** Netmask: <NETMASK> Example shown: 24
** Gateway: <DefGW-IPAddr> Example shown: 172.18.161.254
** DNS Server: <DNS-SV-IPAddr> Example shown: 8.8.8.8

image::media/media/8.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Click Done.
+
--
image::media/media/9.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Set the root password.
+
--
image::media/media/10.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Select User Creation. Here, we will create a user named cloud-user as an example.
+
--
image::media/media/11.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Set the username and password, check "Make this user administrator", and click Done.
+
--
image::media/media/12.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Click Begin Installation.
+
--
image::media/media/13.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Installation begins: Wait for a while.
+
--
image::media/media/14.jpg[width=1000]
--
. When installation is complete, click Reboot System.
+
--
image::media/media/15.jpg[width=1000]
--
. If the login screen appears, the installation is complete.
+
--
image::media/media/image21.png[width=1000]
--

==== MAAS Server Construction

This procedure is performed on the server that will be the MAAS server.
--
image::media/media/image_maincomponent_maas.png[width=1000]
--
For the MAAS server construction, install snap, lxd, and maas in that order.

===== snap Installation

. Add and enable the EPEL repository.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo dnf install https://dl.fedoraproject.org/pub/epel/epel-release-latest-9.noarch.rpm
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(omitted)
Installed:
  epel-release-9-10.el9.noarch

Complete!
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo dnf repolist all | grep epel
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
epel                                                          Extra Packa Enabled
----
--

. Install snapd.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo dnf install snapd
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(omitted)
Installed:
  snap-confine-2.74.1-1.el9.x86_64    snapd-2.74.1-1.el9.x86_64    snapd-selinux-2.74.1-1.el9.noarch    xdelta-3.1.0-17.el9.x86_64

Complete!
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo dnf list snapd
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Installed packages
snapd.x86_64                                                    2.74.1-1.el9                                                    @epel
----
--

. Enable and immediately start snapd.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo systemctl enable --now snapd.socket
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Created symlink /etc/systemd/system/sockets.target.wants/snapd.socket → /usr/lib/systemd/system/snapd.socket.
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
Not required in this step; will be verified in a later step
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Not required in this step; will be verified in a later step
----
--

. Create a symbolic link from /var/lib/snapd/snap to /snap.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo ln -sf /var/lib/snapd/snap /snap
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ ls -l /snap
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
lrwxrwxrwx 1 root root 19  Apr 17 15:32 /snap -> /var/lib/snapd/snap/
----
--

. Create a shell script (snap.sh) that adds /snap/bin to PATH.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ cat << 'EOF' | sudo tee snap.sh
# shellcheck shell=sh
# Expand $PATH to include the directory where snappy applications go.
snap_bin_path="/snap/bin"
if [ -n "${PATH##*${snap_bin_path}}" ] && [ -n "${PATH##*${snap_bin_path}:*}" ]; then
    export PATH="$PATH:${snap_bin_path}"
fi
EOF
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----

fi

fi

===== Manager Network Subnet DHCP Configuration

To deploy OS with MAAS, PXE boot is required, and for this purpose, DHCP is enabled on the Manager Network Subnet.

. First, log in to the MAAS server GUI. The Username and Password are the ones configured with maas createadmin in the previous section.

  URL:http://<MAAS IPAddr>:5240/MAAS/r
--
image::media/media/image57.jpg[width=1000]
--

. After logging in, the initial setup screen will appear. Press "Skip (to user) setup" several times to complete the initial setup.
+
--
image::media/media/image58.jpg[width=1000]
image::media/media/image59.jpg[width=1000]
image::media/media/image60.jpg[width=1000]
--

. First, enable DHCP itself. Icons will be added on the left side. Click Subnet among them, then click the VLAN (untagged) of the manager network subnet (<MGR-Subnet> ("172.31.31.0/24" in the figure example)).
+
--
image::media/media/image69.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Click Configure DHCP.
+
--
image::media/media/image70.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Select the MAAS server in Rack controller, select the manager network subnet (<MGR-Subnet> ("172.31.31.0/24" in the figure example)) in Select subnet, then click Configure DHCP.
+
--
image::media/media/image74.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Then, on the Subnet screen, the manager network DHCP will change from "No DHCP" to "MAAS-provided".
+
--
image::media/media/image75.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Next, configure DHCP settings. Click Subnet, then click the manager network subnet (<MGR-Subnet> ("172.31.31.0/24" in the figure example)).
+
--
image::media/media/image61.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Click Address reservation.
+
--
image::media/media/image62.jpg[width=1000]
--

. First, configure Static DHCP leases. This registers pairs of MAC addresses of each server's manager network NIC and fixed IP addresses, ensuring that fixed IP addresses are always assigned. +
  Click the Reserve static DHCP lease button.
+
--
image::media/media/image63.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Register the IP address and MAC address pair (Comment is optional). Then click the Reserve static DHCP lease button.
+
--
image::media/media/image64.jpg[width=1000]
--

. With this, for example, the server with MAC address=aa.bb.cc.dd.ee.ff will always be assigned IP address=172.31.31.31.
+
--
image::media/media/image65.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Next, configure Reserved ranges. This sets the "excluded range" of the IP address pool for MAAS to automatically assign IP addresses to VM manager network NICs. +
  First, delete the Reserved dynamic range configured in Configure DHCP as it is not needed. (Click the trash can button.)
+
--
image::media/media/image76.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Next, click the Reserve range > Reserve range button.
+
--
image::media/media/image66.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Set Start IP address and End IP address, then click Reserve. +
For example, if you want the IP address pool range for VM manager network NICs to be 172.31.31.41-172.31.31.49, set the following in "Reserve range". +
　(1) 172.31.31.1-172.31.31.40 +
　(2) 172.31.31.50-172.31.31.251 +
　(3) 172.31.31.253-172.31.31.254 +
  *GW IP (e.g., 172.31.31.252) cannot be included in "Reserve range", so configure it by excluding as shown above. +
  *IP addresses registered in "Static DHCP leases" can be included in the "Reserve range" without any issues.
+
--
image::media/media/image67.jpg[width=1000]
--

. With this, VM manager network NICs will be assigned from the range 172.31.31.41-172.31.31.49.
+
--
image::media/media/image68.jpg[width=1000]
--

===== RHEL Image Creation for MAAS Registration

. Create a MAAS image using Packer.​ (*Example of creation on Ubuntu)
** Packer must be installed
*** Prerequisite: Ubuntu version 22.04.5
*** Reference: https://developer.hashicorp.com/packer/tutorials/docker-get-started/get-started-install-cli
** qemu-related packages must be installed
*** Example on Ubuntu: sudo apt install libnbd-bin nbdkit fuse2fs qemu-utils​

. Create RHEL 9.6 image for MAAS
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ git clone https://github.com/canonical/packer-maas.git
$ cd packer-maas/rhel9
$ vi rhel9.pkr.hcl
　packer {
　　  required_version = ">= 1.7.0"
  　　required_plugins {
    　　qemu = {
      　　　version = "1.1.1"    * Change this
     ：
  source "qemu" "rhel9" {
    disk_size        =  "6G"  * Change this

$ export ISO=/XXXX/rhel-9.6-x86_64-dvd.iso   <- Set the path to the rhel-9.6-x86_dvd.iso image file (rhel-<RHEL Version>-x86_64-dvd.iso)
$ sudo -E make
  Approximately 15 minutes
　：
rhel9.tar.gz will be created; rename it to, for example, rhel9_6.tar.gz for use
----
--

===== RHEL Image Registration to MAAS
. Store the created MAAS RHEL image (rhel9_6.tar.gz) on the MAAS server.

. Log in to the MAAS server and execute the following at the location where the RHEL image is stored.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo /snap/bin/maas apikey --username $PROFILE(admin) … ①​
$ maas login $PROFILE(admin) http://localhost:5240/MAAS/<API key displayed in step 1>
$ maas $PROFILE(admin) boot-resources create name='rhel/9.6-custom' title='RHEL 9.6 Custom' architecture='amd64/generic' filetype='tgz' content@=rhel9_6.tar.gz 
----
--

. MAAS GUI image after registration
+
--
image::media/media/image54.png[width=1000]
--

===== Reference

To uninstall MAAS, execute the following. +
To reinstall, re-execute from 3.2.2.3.

[source,console]
.Execution Procedure
----
$ sudo snap remove maas maas-test-db
----

=== DCI Management Cluster Construction

This section describes the procedure for constructing a Kubernetes cluster to be used as the DCI management cluster. +
Specifically, it explains the installation and configuration of Kubernetes and CRI-O, the prerequisites for cluster construction, and the procedure for constructing the Kubernetes cluster.

This section shows the procedures corresponding to the area enclosed in the red frame in 2.4. Construction Flow.

image::media/media/flow3.png[width=432]

Also, the target servers correspond to the area enclosed in the red frame below in the physical server configuration of the construction target scope.
--
image::media/media/image_maincomponent_k8s.png[width=1000]
--

.Rollback Procedure
If a problem occurs in the procedure of each section in this chapter, in principle, redo the procedure of the relevant section from the beginning. +
However, if a rollback method or re-execution starting point is explicitly stated within individual procedures, follow those instructions.

==== K8s Installation

. If swap is configured, kubelet will fail to start, so disable it.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
# sudo swapoff -a
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ free
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
               total        used        free      shared  buff/cache   available
Mem:       527379364   110580648    25103164     3244048   398843308   416798716
Swap:              0           0           0
----
--

. Make the swap disable setting permanent.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudoedit /etc/fstab
  #/dev/mapper/rhel-swap   none                    swap    defaults        0 0    
  ^ Comment out (add # at the beginning of the line)
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----

(omitted)

. Set the K8S version and configure the K8S repository. Here is an example with v1.34.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ K8S_VERSION=v1.34
($ K8S_VERSION=<K8S Version>)
$ cat <<EOF | sudo tee /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo
[kubernetes]
name=Kubernetes
baseurl=https://pkgs.k8s.io/core:/stable:/$K8S_VERSION/rpm/
enabled=1
gpgcheck=1
gpgkey=https://pkgs.k8s.io/core:/stable:/$K8S_VERSION/rpm/repodata/repomd.xml.key
EOF
----

==== CRI-O Installation

. Set the CRI-O version and configure the CRI-O repository. Here is an example with v1.34.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ CRIO_VERSION=v1.34
($ CRIO_VERSION=<CRI-O Version)
$ cat <<EOF | sudo tee /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo
[cri-o]
name=CRI-O
baseurl=https://download.opensuse.org/repositories/isv:/cri-o:/stable:/$CRIO_VERSION/rpm/
enabled=1
gpgcheck=1
gpgkey=https://download.opensuse.org/repositories/isv:/cri-o:/stable:/$CRIO_VERSION/rpm/repodata/repomd.xml.key
EOF
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]

[cri-o]
name=CRI-O
baseurl=https://download.opensuse.org/repositories/isv:/cri-o:/stable:/v1.34/rpm/
enabled=1
gpgcheck=1
gpgkey=https://download.opensuse.org/repositories/isv:/cri-o:/stable:/v1.34/rpm/repodata/repomd.xml.key
----

==== CRI-O Configuration

. Change the settings so that the subnet used by CRI-O does not overlap with subnets used by other software.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudoedit /etc/cni/net.d/10-crio-bridge.conflist.disabled
    "ranges": [
        [{ "subnet": "172.85.0.0/16" }], (Change the subnet address)
        [{ "subnet": "1100:200::/24" }]
    ]
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ more /etc/cni/net.d/10-crio-bridge.conflist.disabled
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(omitted)
    "ranges": [
        [{ "subnet": "172.85.0.0/16" }],
        [{ "subnet": "1100:200::/24" }]
    ]
----
--

. Back up the CRI-O bridge as Calico will be used as the CNI.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ cd /etc/cni/net.d
$ sudo mkdir /etc/crio/net.d
$ sudo mv 10-crio-bridge.conflist.disabled /etc/crio/net.d/
$ sudo rm -rf /etc/cni/net.d/
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ more /etc/crio/net.d/10-crio-bridge.conflist.disabled
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(omitted)
    "ranges": [
        [{ "subnet": "172.85.0.0/16" }],
        [{ "subnet": "1100:200::/24" }]
    ]
----
--

. Restart the daemon, enable CRI-O, and start it immediately.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo systemctl daemon-reload
$ sudo systemctl enable crio
$ sudo systemctl start crio
----

.Execution Result

----

$ curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/projectcalico/calico/v3.29.2/manifests/calico.yaml -O
($ curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/projectcalico/calico/<Calico Version>/manifests/calico.yaml -O)

  % Total    % Received % Xferd  Average Speed   Time    Time     Time  Current

100  316k  100  316k    0     0  1168k      0 --:--:-- --:--:-- --:--:-- 1168k

[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----
--

. Edit calico.yaml. Specifically, search for “autedetect” and add the following content immediately after it.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ vi calico.yaml
  # Auto-detect the BGP IP address.
  - name: IP
    value: “autodetect”
  # Set Auto-Detection Method of Network Interface * Add the following; this line is a comment and does not necessarily need to be added
  - name: IP_AUTODETECTION_METHOD
    value: kubernetes-internal-ip
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ more calico.yaml
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(omitted)
  # Auto-detect the BGP IP address.
  - name: IP
    value: “autodetect”
  # Set Auto-Detection Method of Network Interface
  - name: IP_AUTODETECTION_METHOD
    value: kubernetes-internal-ip
----
--

==== K8s Cluster Construction
The content of this section assumes an environment where the Kubernetes Control Plane is configured with 3 Nodes for redundancy.

===== Work on the 1st control plane

. Create kube-vip.yaml and place it in /etc/kubernetes/manifests/.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ vi kube-vip.yaml (configuration content is as follows)
$ sudo mv kube-vip.yaml /etc/kubernetes/manifests/
----
[source,bash]
----
apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: kube-vip
  namespace: kube-system
spec:
  containers:
  - args:
    - manager
    env:
    - name: vip_arp
      value: "true"
    - name: port
      value: "6443"
    - name: vip_interface
      value: eno0            * Specify the manager network interface according to the environment
    - name: vip_cidr
      value: "24"
    - name: cp_enable
      value: "true"
    - name: cp_namespace
      value: kube-system
    - name: vip_ddns
      value: "false"
    - name: svc_enable
      value: "true"
    - name: vip_leaderelection
      value: "true"
    - name: vip_leaseduration
      value: "5"
    - name: vip_renewdeadline
      value: "3"
    - name: vip_retryperiod
      value: "1"
    - name: address
      value: "172.18.193.30"  * Specify an unused virtual VIP address (<MGR-IPAddr-VIP>)
    image: ghcr.io/kube-vip/kube-vip:v0.8.4
    imagePullPolicy: Always
    name: kube-vip
    resources: {}
    securityContext:
      capabilities:
        add:
        - NET_ADMIN
        - NET_RAW
    volumeMounts:
    - mountPath: /etc/kubernetes/admin.conf
      name: kubeconfig
  hostAliases:
  - hostnames:
    - kubernetes
    ip: 127.0.0.1
  hostNetwork: true
  volumes:
  - hostPath:
      path: /etc/kubernetes/super-admin.conf　* For the 2nd Node onward, remove "super-"
    name: kubeconfig
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
more /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-vip.yaml
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: kube-vip
  namespace: kube-system
spec:
  containers:
  - args:
    - manager
    env:
    - name: vip_arp
      value: "true"
    - name: port
      value: "6443"
    - name: vip_interface
      value: eno1
    - name: vip_cidr
      value: "24"
    - name: cp_enable
      value: "true"
    - name: cp_namespace
      value: kube-system
    - name: vip_ddns
      value: "false"
    - name: svc_enable
      value: "true"
    - name: vip_leaderelection
      value: "true"
    - name: vip_leaseduration
      value: "5"
    - name: vip_renewdeadline
      value: "3"
    - name: vip_retryperiod
      value: "1"
    - name: address
      value: "172.18.193.30"(<MGR-IPAddr-VIP>)
    image: ghcr.io/kube-vip/kube-vip:v0.8.4
    imagePullPolicy: Always
    name: kube-vip
    resources: {}
    securityContext:
      capabilities:
        add:
        - NET_ADMIN
        - NET_RAW
    volumeMounts:
    - mountPath: /etc/kubernetes/admin.conf
      name: kubeconfig
  hostAliases:
  - hostnames:
    - kubernetes
    ip: 127.0.0.1
  hostNetwork: true
  volumes:
  - hostPath:
      path: /etc/kubernetes/super-admin.conf
    name: kubeconfig
----
--

. Create kubeadm-config.yaml and place it in /etc/kubernetes/.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ vi kubeadm-config.yaml (configuration content is as follows)
$ sudo mv kubeadm-config.yaml /etc/kubernetes/
----
[source,bash]
----
apiVersion: kubeadm.k8s.io/v1beta4
kind: InitConfiguration
localAPIEndpoint:
  advertiseAddress: "172.18.193.10"  * Manager Network interface address (<MGR-IPAddr-CP1>)
  bindPort: 6443
nodeRegistration:

  imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
  imagePullSerial: true
  name: "d1-f01-k8s-master01"        * Hostname (<mgr-master01>)
  taints: null

  podSubnet: "182.168.0.0/16"
  serviceSubnet: "10.96.0.0/12"

clusterName: kubernetes
certificatesDir: /etc/kubernetes/pki

controllerManager: {}
scheduler: {}
dns: {}
proxy: {}
caCertificateValidityPeriod: 87600h0m0s
certificateValidityPeriod: 8760h0m0s

===== Work on the 2nd and 3rd control planes

. Create kube-vip.yaml and place it in /etc/kubernetes/manifests/.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ vi kube-vip.yaml (settings are as follows)
$ sudo mv kube-vip.yaml /etc/kubernetes/manifests/
----
[source,bash]
----
apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: kube-vip
  namespace: kube-system
spec:
  containers:
  - args:
    - manager
    env:
    - name: vip_arp
      value: "true"
    - name: port
      value: "6443"
    - name: vip_interface
      value: eno0            * Specify the manager network interface according to the environment
    - name: vip_cidr
      value: "24"
    - name: cp_enable
      value: "true"
    - name: cp_namespace
      value: kube-system
    - name: vip_ddns
      value: "false"
    - name: svc_enable
      value: "true"
    - name: vip_leaderelection
      value: "true"
    - name: vip_leaseduration
      value: "5"
    - name: vip_renewdeadline
      value: "3"
    - name: vip_retryperiod
      value: "1"
    - name: address
      value: "172.18.193.30"  * Specify an unused virtual VIP address (<MGR-IPAddr-VIP>:6443)
    image: ghcr.io/kube-vip/kube-vip:v0.8.4
    imagePullPolicy: Always
    name: kube-vip
    resources: {}
    securityContext:
      capabilities:
        add:
        - NET_ADMIN
        - NET_RAW
    volumeMounts:
    - mountPath: /etc/kubernetes/admin.conf
      name: kubeconfig
  hostAliases:
  - hostnames:
    - kubernetes
    ip: 127.0.0.1
  hostNetwork: true
  volumes:
  - hostPath:
      path: /etc/kubernetes/admin.conf　* For the 2nd Node onward, remove "super-"
    name: kubeconfig
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

[source,console]
----
more /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-vip.yaml
----

        --cri-socket=unix:///var/run/crio/crio.sock
($ sudo kubeadm join <MGR-IPAddr-VIP>:6443 --token 3ufeb5.k9y57d74jess1q72 \

. Note that join may occasionally fail. In that case, delete the settings using the following procedure and redo from the work on the 2nd and 3rd control planes.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo kubeadm reset
$ sudo rm -rf /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml
$ sudo rm -rf /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-controller-manager.yaml
$ sudo rm -rf /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-scheduler.yaml
$ sudo rm -rf /etc/kubernetes/manifests/etcd.yaml
$ sudo rm -rf /var/lib/etcd
$ sudo reboot
----

=== Preparation for DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure Deployment

This section describes the preparatory steps for deploying the DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure. +
Specifically, it describes the installation procedures for helm, git, buildah, go, and yq, as well as the NTP server configuration procedure.

This section shows the procedures corresponding to the parts enclosed in red frames in 2.4. Construction Flow.

image::media/media/flow4.png[width=432]

==== helm Installation `@control plane*`
Execute on "all" control planes used for the DCI management cluster.

. Install wget.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo yum install wget

=== DCI Manager Deployment

This section describes the procedures for deploying DCI Manager-related components that manage the entire DCI.

This section shows the procedures corresponding to the parts enclosed in red frames in 2.4. Construction Flow.

image::media/media/flow5.png[width=432]

==== Environment Variable Configuration for Specifying Material Versions `@control plane`, `@k8s Node`

. Set the environment variables for specifying material versions.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ echo "export TAG_MANAGER=0.4.2" >> ~/.bashrc
$ echo "export TAG_PHYSICAL=0.3.1" >> ~/.bashrc
$ echo "export TAG_TENANT=0.3.1" >> ~/.bashrc
$ echo "export TAG_COMMON=0.5.1" >> ~/.bashrc
$ source ~/.bashrc

==== etcd Secret Encryption `@control plane`

. Create the "encryption configuration file (encryption-config.yaml)" to prevent Kubernetes Secrets from being stored in plaintext on etcd.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ echo '{"apiVersion":"apiserver.config.k8s.io/v1","kind":"EncryptionConfiguration","resources":[{"resources":["secrets"],"providers":[{"aescbc":{"keys":[{"name":"key1"}]}},{"identity":{}}]}]}' | sudo --preserve-env=PATH sh -c 'yq e -P > /etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml'
$ head -c 32 /dev/urandom | base64 | tr -d '\n' | sudo --preserve-env=PATH sh -c 'xargs -I {} yq -i e ".resources[0].providers[0].aescbc.keys[0].secret = \"{}\"" /etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml'
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ more /etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
apiVersion: apiserver.config.k8s.io/v1
kind: EncryptionConfiguration
resources:
  - resources:
      - secrets
    providers:
      - aescbc:
          keys:
            - name: key1
              secret: SveQ9G2f59ptzdwYGE8MpGoJplBf9fLY79qL6WFiQ9o=
      - identity: {}
----
--

. Mount the created encryption-config.yaml to the kube-apiserver container and enable Secret encryption with that configuration.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo --preserve-env=PATH sh -c "yq -i '.spec.volumes += [{\"hostPath\":{\"path\":\"/etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml\", \"type\":\"File\"}, \"name\":\"encryption-config\"}]' /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml"
$ sudo --preserve-env=PATH sh -c "yq -i '.spec.containers[0].volumeMounts += [{\"mountPath\":\"/etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml\", \"name\":\"encryption-config\", \"readOnly\":true}]' /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml"
$ sudo --preserve-env=PATH sh -c "yq -i '.spec.containers[0].command += [\"--encryption-provider-config=/etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml\"]' /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml"

----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----
--

. Rewrite all Secrets in the cluster to be encrypted.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl get secrets --all-namespaces -o json | kubectl replace -f -

----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
secret/bootstrap-token-c29kwr replaced
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
sudo grep encryption-provider-config /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
        - --encryption-provider-config=/etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml
----
--

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
/etc/kubernetes/audit_policy.yaml
----
--

. Configure kube-apiserver.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudoedit /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml
(1) Add the following to spec.containers.command
---------------------------------------------------------------
        - --audit-policy-file=/etc/kubernetes/audit_policy.yaml
        - --audit-log-path=/var/log/kubernetes/audit/audit.log
---------------------------------------------------------------
(2) Add the following to spec.containers.volumeMounts
---------------------------------------------------------------
        - mountPath: /etc/kubernetes/audit_policy.yaml
          name: audit
          readOnly: true
        - mountPath: /var/log/kubernetes/audit/
          name: audit-log
          readOnly: false
---------------------------------------------------------------
(3) Add the following to spec.volumes
---------------------------------------------------------------
    - hostPath:
        path: /etc/kubernetes/audit_policy.yaml
        type: File
      name: audit
    - hostPath:
        path: /var/log/kubernetes/audit/
        type: DirectoryOrCreate
      name: audit-log
---------------------------------------------------------------
----

[source,console]
----

-a always,exit -F arch=b64 -S open,bind,truncate,ftruncate,rename,mkdir,rmdir,creat,link,unlink,symlink,chmod,fchmod,chown,fchown,lchown,mknod,acct,swapon,quotactl,setxattr,lsetxattr,fsetxattr,removexattr,lremovexattr,fremovexattr,openat,mkdirat,mknodat,fchownat,unlinkat,renameat,linkat,symlinkat,fchmodat,fallocate,renameat2,openat2 -F perm=wa -F path=/etc/sudoers -F key=sudo
-a always,exit -F arch=b64 -S open,bind,truncate,ftruncate,rename,mkdir,rmdir,creat,link,unlink,symlink,chmod,fchmod,chown,fchown,lchown,mknod,acct,swapon,quotactl,setxattr,lsetxattr,fsetxattr,removexattr,lremovexattr,fremovexattr,openat,mkdirat,mknodat,fchownat,unlinkat,renameat,linkat,symlinkat,fchmodat,fallocate,renameat2,openat2 -F perm=wa -F path=/etc/sudoers.d -F key=sudo

. Edit gateway-lb.yaml.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ vi gateway-lb.yaml
* The 172.18.193.31 (2 locations) in the file sets the VIP for LoadBalancer type Services. Note: this is NOT the control plane VIP set in the kube-vip manifest. Set an available IP address in the manager network subnet.
----

[source,bash]
----
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: gateway-lb
  namespace: nginx-gateway
  annotations:
    kube-vip.io/loadbalancerIPs: "172.18.193.31"(<LB-Service-VIP>)
spec:
  type: LoadBalancer
  loadBalancerIP: 172.18.193.31(<LB-Service-VIP>)
  selector:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: gateway-nginx
  ports:
    - name: http
      port: 80
      targetPort: 80
      protocol: TCP
    - name: https
      port: 8443
      targetPort: 8443
      protocol: TCP
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]

No output
----

[source,console]

$ more gateway-lb.yaml

. Edit vip-cert.yaml.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ vi vip-cert.yaml
* The 172.18.193.31 in the file sets the VIP for LoadBalancer type Services. Note: this is NOT the control plane VIP set in the kube-vip manifest. Set an available IP address in the manager network subnet.
----

[source,bash]
----
apiVersion: cert-manager.io/v1
kind: Certificate
metadata:
  name: vip-cert
  namespace: nginx-gateway
spec:
  secretName: vip-tls
  issuerRef:
    name: gateway-ca-issuer
    kind: Issuer
  dnsNames:
    - loki.local
    - db-api.local
  ipAddresses:
    - 172.18.193.31(<LB-Service-VIP>)
----

$ cd ~/dci_manager/others/harbor

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
expose:
  type: nodePort
  tls:
    enabled: true
    certSource: auto
    auto:
      commonName: 172.18.193.30 #<MGR-IPAddr-VIP> *Modify configuration values according to the environment
  nodePort:
    name: harbor
    ports:
      http:
        port: 80
        nodePort: 30002
      https:
        port: 443
        nodePort: 30003
externalURL: https://172.18.193.30:30003 #<MGR-IPAddr-VIP> IP *Modify configuration values according to the environment
persistence:
  enabled: true
  persistentVolumeClaim:
    registry:
      storageClass: nfs-client
      accessMode: ReadWriteOnce
    jobservice:
      jobLog:
        storageClass: nfs-client
        accessMode: ReadWriteOnce
    database:
      storageClass: nfs-client
      accessMode: ReadWriteOnce
    redis:
      storageClass: nfs-client
      accessMode: ReadWriteOnce
    trivy:
      storageClass: nfs-client
      accessMode: ReadWriteOnce

nginx:
  nodeSelector:
    node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""
  tolerations:
    - key: node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane
      effect: NoSchedule

core:
  nodeSelector:
    node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""
  tolerations:
    - key: node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane
      effect: NoSchedule

portal:
  nodeSelector:
    node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""
  tolerations:
    - key: node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane
      effect: NoSchedule

jobservice:
  nodeSelector:
    node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""
  tolerations:
    - key: node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane
      effect: NoSchedule

registry:
  nodeSelector:
    node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""
  tolerations:
    - key: node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane
      effect: NoSchedule

trivy:
  nodeSelector:
    node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""
  tolerations:
    - key: node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane
      effect: NoSchedule

database:
  internal:
    nodeSelector:
      node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""
    tolerations:
      - key: node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane
        effect: NoSchedule

redis:
  internal:
    nodeSelector:
      node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""
    tolerations:

--

. Deploy Harbor.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
helm upgrade --install harbor harbor/harbor \
            --version 1.18.1 \
            -n harbor \
            -f values.yaml \
            --set nginx.image.repository=docker.io/goharbor/nginx-photon \
            --set portal.image.repository=docker.io/goharbor/harbor-portal \
            --set core.image.repository=docker.io/goharbor/harbor-core \
            --set jobservice.image.repository=docker.io/goharbor/harbor-jobservice \
            --set registry.registry.image.repository=docker.io/goharbor/registry-photon \
            --set registry.controller.image.repository=docker.io/goharbor/harbor-registryctl \
            --set trivy.image.repository=docker.io/goharbor/trivy-adapter-photon \

==== Material Storage to Harbor

. Prepare for material storage.

.. Set environment variables. `@control plane`
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~
$ echo "export HARBOR_MNG=<harbor-url>" >> ~/.bashrc  * <harbor-url>: Manager-side Harbor IP address (kube-vip IP address of the cluster where Harbor Pod is deployed in the DCI management cluster)
$ source ~/.bashrc
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

$ more .bashrc
----

.Verification Result

(omitted)
export HARBOR_MNG=172.18.193.30
----

.. Perform certificate authentication. `@all Nodes (control plane and k8s Nodes)`
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl get secrets -n harbor harbor-nginx -o jsonpath='{.data.ca\.crt}' | base64 -d > harbor_mng.crt
$ sudo cp harbor_mng.crt /etc/ssl/certs/
$ sudo update-ca-trust
$ sudo systemctl restart crio
$ sudo cp harbor_mng.crt /etc/pki/ca-trust/source/anchors/
$ sudo update-ca-trust extract
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output

.. Log in to Harbor and create projects. `@control plane`
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo buildah login -u **username** -p **password** $HARBOR_MNG:30003
$ curl -X POST https://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/api/v2.0/projects -H "accept: application/json" -H "Content-Type: application/json" -u "**username**:**password**" -d '{ "project_name": "dci_manager", "public": true, "metadata": {"auto_scan": "true"}}'
$ curl -X POST https://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/api/v2.0/projects -H "accept: application/json" -H "Content-Type: application/json" -u "**username**:**password**" -d '{ "project_name": "dci_physical_infrastructure", "public": true, "metadata": {"auto_scan": "true"}}'
$ curl -X POST https://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/api/v2.0/projects -H "accept: application/json" -H "Content-Type: application/json" -u "**username**:**password**" -d '{ "project_name": "dci_tenant_infrastructure", "public": true, "metadata": {"auto_scan": "true"}}'

.. Build the resource pool management materials as two types, "container image" and "Helm chart", and register them to Harbor.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~/dci_manager/resource-pool-manager/
$ sudo make buildah-build IMG=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/resource-pool-manager:${TAG_MANAGER} ALERT_RECEIVER_IMG=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/rpm-alert-receiver:${TAG_MANAGER}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/resource-pool-manager:${TAG_MANAGER}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/rpm-alert-receiver:${TAG_MANAGER}
$ make helmchart 
$ helm push ./dist/resource-pool-manager-v${TAG_MANAGER}.tgz oci://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(Verify that the application container images for resource-pool-manager and rpm-alert-receiver are built and registered with the specified tag in the dci_manager project of the Harbor registry)
(Verify that the Helm chart package for deploying resource-pool-manager to the Kubernetes environment is generated and registered in the OCI Helm Chart repository of the Harbor registry)

.. Build the physical infrastructure materials as two types, "container image" and "Helm chart", and register them to Harbor.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~
$ git clone -b v${TAG_PHYSICAL} https://github.com/compsysg/dci_physical_infrastructure
$ cd dci_physical_infrastructure
$ make build-all IMG_TAG=${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah tag localhost/dci_physical_infrastructure/cdi:${TAG_PHYSICAL} $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/cdi:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah tag localhost/dci_physical_infrastructure/maas:${TAG_PHYSICAL} $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/maas:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah tag localhost/dci_physical_infrastructure/network:${TAG_PHYSICAL} $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/network:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah tag localhost/dci_physical_infrastructure/exporter:${TAG_PHYSICAL} $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/exporter:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah tag localhost/dci_physical_infrastructure/log:${TAG_PHYSICAL} $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/log:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/cdi:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/maas:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/network:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/exporter:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/log:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ helm package ./chart/
$ helm push physical-infrastructure-v${TAG_PHYSICAL}.tgz oci://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/
----

[source,console]

. Store tenant provisioning materials. `@control plane`

.. Build dcgm-exporter as a "container image" and register it to Harbor.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~
$ git clone -b 4.4.2-4.7.0 https://github.com/NVIDIA/dcgm-exporter.git
($ git clone -b <DCGM-exporter Version> https://github.com/NVIDIA/dcgm-exporter.git)
$ git clone -b v${TAG_TENANT} https://github.com/compsysg/dci_tenant_infrastructure
$ cp ./dci_tenant_infrastructure/app-monitor-dci/gpu-operator/dra_mig_corefix.patch ~/dcgm-exporter/
$ cd ~/dcgm-exporter/
$ git apply dra_mig_corefix.patch
$ sudo buildah bud --format docker --file docker/Dockerfile --target runtime-ubi --tag $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_tenant_infrastructure/dcgm-exporter:4.4.2-4.7.0-corefix --build-arg GOLANG_VERSION=1.24.5 --build-arg VERSION=4.7.0 --build-arg DCGM_VERSION=4.4.2 .
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_tenant_infrastructure/dcgm-exporter:4.4.2-4.7.0-corefix
($ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_tenant_infrastructure/dcgm-exporter:<DCGM-exporter Version>-corefix)
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(Verify that the NVIDIA dcgm-exporter source code and dci_tenant_infrastructure repository are retrieved, the dra_mig_corefix.patch provided by dci_tenant_infrastructure is applied to dcgm-exporter, and the modified dcgm-exporter container image is registered with the 4.4.2-4.7.0-corefix tag in the dci_tenant_infrastructure project of the Harbor registry)

NOTES:
kube-prometheus-stack has been installed. Check its status by running:
  kubectl --namespace prometheus get pods -l "release=prometheus"

Get Grafana 'admin' user password by running:

  kubectl --namespace prometheus get secrets prometheus-grafana -o jsonpath="{.data.admin-password}" | base64 -d ; echo

Access Grafana local instance:

  export POD_NAME=$(kubectl --namespace prometheus get pod -l "app.kubernetes.io/name=grafana,app.kubernetes.io/instance=prometheus" -oname)
  kubectl --namespace prometheus port-forward $POD_NAME 3000

Get your grafana admin user password by running:

  kubectl get secret --namespace prometheus -l app.kubernetes.io/component=admin-secret -o jsonpath="{.items[0].data.admin-password}" | base64 --decode ; echo


Visit https://github.com/prometheus-operator/kube-prometheus for instructions on how to create & configure Alertmanager and Prometheus instances using the Operator.
----

.Verification Procedure

$ helm list -n prometheus

NAME: loki
LAST DEPLOYED: Thu May  7 15:56:03 2026
NAMESPACE: loki
STATUS: deployed
REVISION: 1
DESCRIPTION: Install complete
NOTES:
***********************************************************************
 Welcome to Grafana Loki
 Chart version: 6.29.0
 Chart Name: loki
 Loki version: 3.4.2
***********************************************************************

** Please be patient while the chart is being deployed **

Tip:

  Watch the deployment status using the command: kubectl get pods -w --namespace loki

If pods are taking too long to schedule make sure pod affinity can be fulfilled in the current cluster.

***********************************************************************
Installed components:
***********************************************************************
* loki

Loki has been deployed as a single binary.
This means a single pod is handling reads and writes. You can scale that pod vertically by adding more CPU and memory resources.


***********************************************************************
Sending logs to Loki
***********************************************************************

Loki has been configured with a gateway (nginx) to support reads and writes from a single component.

You can send logs from inside the cluster using the cluster DNS:

http://loki-gateway.loki.svc.cluster.local/loki/api/v1/push

You can test to send data from outside the cluster by port-forwarding the gateway to your local machine:

  kubectl port-forward --namespace loki svc/loki-gateway 3100:80 &

And then using http://127.0.0.1:3100/loki/api/v1/push URL as shown below:

```
curl -H "Content-Type: application/json" -XPOST -s "http://127.0.0.1:3100/loki/api/v1/push"  \
--data-raw "{\"streams\": [{\"stream\": {\"job\": \"test\"}, \"values\": [[\"$(date +%s)000000000\", \"fizzbuzz\"]]}]}"
```

Then verify that Loki did receive the data using the following command:

```
curl "http://127.0.0.1:3100/loki/api/v1/query_range" --data-urlencode 'query={job="test"}' | jq .data.result
```

***********************************************************************
Connecting Grafana to Loki
***********************************************************************

If Grafana operates within the cluster, you'll set up a new Loki datasource by utilizing the following URL:

http://loki-gateway.loki.svc.cluster.local/

.. Configure rsyslog.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo mkdir -p /var/log/mass/audit
$ sudo mkdir -p /var/log/cdi/audit
$ sudo mkdir -p /var/log/network_sw/audit
$ sudoedit /etc/rsyslog.d/audit_machine_ips.lst
* Based on the following examples, change the content within table[] according to the monitoring target devices.
    MAAS server: "maas"
    CDI management server, Liqid Director, Client Linux-PC: "cdi"
    SONiC (network equipment): "network_sw"
* Do not use comments as they may cause errors.
----

[source,bash]
----
{
  "version": 1,
  "table": [
    { "index": "1.1.21.1", "value": "maas" },
    { "index": "1.1.22.2", "value": "maas" },
    { "index": "1.1.23.3", "value": "maas" },
    { "index": "2.1.11.1", "value": "network_sw" },
    { "index": "2.1.12.2", "value": "network_sw" },
    { "index": "3.1.77.1", "value": "cdi" },
    { "index": "4.1.88.1", "value": "cdi" },
    { "index": "5.1.99.1", "value": "cdi" }
  ]
}
----

[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----
--

.. Edit /etc/rsyslog.conf.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudoedit /etc/rsyslog.conf
* Add the following to the bottom of the file.
----

[source,bash]
----
module(load="imtcp")
input(type="imtcp" port="514" ruleset="test")
if $fromhost-ip == "127.0.0.1" then stop
if $fromhost-ip == "::1" then stop

lookup_table(
  name="audit_machine_iplist"
  file="/etc/rsyslog.d/audit_machine_ips.lst"
  reloadOnHUP="on"
)

if lookup("audit_machine_iplist", $fromhost-ip) != ""  then {
    set $!lk_result = lookup("audit_machine_iplist", $fromhost-ip);
    $template LookupBasedLogs,"/var/log/%$!lk_result%/audit/%fromhost-ip%_%$year%%$month%%$day%.log"
    *.* ?LookupBasedLogs
    stop
}

$template RemoteLogs,"/var/log/network_sw/%fromhost-ip%_%$year%%$month%%$day%.log"
*.* ?RemoteLogs
& ~
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
No output

($ helm upgrade --install cnpg --namespace cnpg-system --create-namespace -f ~/dci_manager/others/configuration-management-database/cnpg-operator/values.yaml --version <CloudNativePG Version> cnpg/cloudnative-pg)

==== CA/Issuer Creation for Physical Infrastructure gRPC mTLS Support `@control plane`

Prerequisite: All custom components of the physical infrastructure (CDI control/MAAS control/network control) use a common CA (Certificate Authority). Even when there are multiple physical infrastructures, they all use the same common CA.

. Create the CA key file and CA crt file.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ mkdir -p ~/pki/shared-root-ca
$ cd ~/pki/shared-root-ca
$ openssl genrsa -out ca.key 4096
$ openssl req -x509 -new -nodes -key ca.key -sha256 -days 3650 \
    -subj "/C=JP/ST=Tokyo/O=Example/OU=Platform/CN=shared-root-ca" \
    -out ca.crt
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----
--

. Create the CA Secret.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl -n cert-manager create secret tls shared-root-ca-keypair \
  --cert=ca.crt \
  --key=ca.key
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
secret/shared-root-ca-keypair created
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----
--

. Create the ClusterIssuer.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ cat <<'EOF' | kubectl apply -f -
apiVersion: cert-manager.io/v1
kind: ClusterIssuer
metadata:
  name: shared-root-ca
spec:
  ca:
    secretName: shared-root-ca-keypair
EOF
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
clusterissuer.cert-manager.io/shared-root-ca created
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
kubectl get clusterissuer shared-root-ca
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
NAME             READY   AGE
shared-root-ca   True    40s
----

==== Resource Pool Management Deployment `@control plane`

. Edit cloud-config.yaml.
+
*Create and apply the following ConfigMap manifest that describes the commands to be executed by cloud-init for VM hosts (server-type: host - fixed as host) and spare Nodes (server-type: spare - fixed as spare) for each location. Describe the commands required for external connectivity such as dnf install.
+
*Modify the nameserver, rhc activation-key/organization, and NTP server according to your environment.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~/dci_manager/resource-pool-manager/
$ vi cloud-config.yaml
----

[source,bash]
----
apiVersion: v1
kind: ConfigMap
metadata:
  name: cloud-config
  namespace: dcirpm
data:
  cloud-configs: |
    - server-type: host
      location: dc1
      body: |
        #cloud-config
        runcmd:
        - "echo 'nameserver 8.8.8.8\nsearch maas' > /etc/resolv.conf" ("echo 'nameserver <DNS-SV-IPAddr>\nsearch maas' > /etc/resolv.conf")
        - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/yum.conf"
        - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/dnf/dnf.conf"
        - "rhc connect --activation-key=xxxx --organization=12345678" ("rhc connect --activation-key=<RHC-activation-key> --organization=<RHC-organization>")
    - server-type: host
      location: dc2
      body: |
        #cloud-config
        runcmd:
        - "echo 'nameserver 8.8.8.8\nsearch maas' > /etc/resolv.conf" ("echo 'nameserver <DNS-SV-IPAddr>\nsearch maas' > /etc/resolv.conf")
        - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/yum.conf"
        - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/dnf/dnf.conf"
        - "rhc connect --activation-key=xxxx --organization=12345678" ("rhc connect --activation-key=<RHC-activation-key> --organization=<RHC-organization>")
    - server-type: spare
      location: dc1
      body: |
        #cloud-config
        runcmd:
        - "echo 'nameserver 8.8.8.8\nsearch maas' > /etc/resolv.conf" ("echo 'nameserver <DNS-SV-IPAddr>\nsearch maas' > /etc/resolv.conf")
        - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/yum.conf"
        - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/dnf/dnf.conf"
        - "rhc connect --activation-key=xxxx --organization=12345678" ("rhc connect --activation-key=<RHC-activation-key> --organization=<RHC-organization>")
        - "dnf install -y wget"
        - "wget https://github.com/mikefarah/yq/releases/latest/download/yq_linux_amd64 -O /usr/bin/yq && chmod +x /usr/bin/yq"
        - "swapoff -a"
        - "sed -i '/swap/ s/^/#/' /etc/fstab"
        - "dnf install -y git"
        - "export KUBERNETES_VERSION=v1.34" ("export KUBERNETES_VERSION=<K8S Version>")
        - "echo [kubernetes] >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
        - "echo name=Kubernetes >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
        - "echo baseurl=https://pkgs.k8s.io/core:/stable:/$KUBERNETES_VERSION/rpm/ >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
        - "echo enabled=1 >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
        - "echo gpgcheck=1 >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
        - "echo gpgkey=https://pkgs.k8s.io/core:/stable:/$KUBERNETES_VERSION/rpm/repodata/repomd.xml.key >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
        - "dnf install -y kubelet-1.34.2-150500.1.1.x86_64 kubeadm-1.34.2-150500.1.1.x86_64 kubectl-1.34.2-150500.1.1.x86_64" ("dnf install -y kubelet-<K8S Package>.x86_64 kubeadm-<K8S Package>.x86_64 kubectl-<K8S Package>.x86_64")
        - "export CRIO_VERSION=v1.34" ("export CRIO_VERSION=<CRI-O Version>")
        - "echo [cri-o] >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
        - "echo name=CRI-O >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
        - "echo baseurl=https://download.opensuse.org/repositories/isv:/cri-o:/stable:/$CRIO_VERSION/rpm/ >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
        - "echo enabled=1 >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
        - "echo gpgcheck=1 >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
        - "echo gpgkey=https://download.opensuse.org/repositories/isv:/cri-o:/stable:/$CRIO_VERSION/rpm/repodata/repomd.xml.key >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
        - "dnf install -y cri-o-1.34.2-150500.1.1.x86_64" ("dnf install -y cri-o-<CRI-O Package>.x86_64")
        - "setenforce 0"
        - "sed -i 's/enforcing/permissive/g' /etc/selinux/config"
        - "dnf install -y nfs-utils-2.5.4-34.el9.x86_64" ("dnf install -y nfs-utils-<NFS-utils Version>.x86_64")
        - "nohup sh -c 'sleep 30; poweroff' &"
        - "echo \"spare node setup done!\""
      ntp:
        enabled: true
        servers:
          - 172.18.197.80 (<NTP-SV-IPAddr>)
----

config:

      port: 5432
      dbname: app
      username: postgres
      secretRef:
        name: database-cluster-app

          port: 82
        nw:
          host: physical-infrastructure-network-service.default.svc.cluster.local
          port: 81
        cdi:
          host: physical-infrastructure-cdi-service.default.svc.cluster.local
          port: 80
    default:
      location: dc1
      os:
        distribution: rhel
        version: "9.6-custom"
      cdiGrpName: group0

==== DB-API (PostgREST) Deployment `@control plane`

. Create a role for DB-API.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl exec -it -n database database-cluster-1 -- bash
postgres@database-cluster-1:/$ psql -U postgres -d app
app=# CREATE ROLE api_user;
app=# GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO api_user;
app=# GRANT CONNECT ON DATABASE app TO api_user;
app=# GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public TO api_user;
app=# exit
postgres@database-cluster-1:/$ exit

$ export DB_PASSWORD=$(kubectl -n database get secrets database-cluster-app -o jsonpath='{.data.password}' | base64 -d)
$ export DB_URI=$(echo -n "postgres://postgres:$DB_PASSWORD@database-cluster-rw.database.svc.cluster.local:5432/app?sslmode=require" | base64 -w 0)
$ export JWT_SECRET=$(< /dev/urandom tr -dc A-Za-z0-9 | head -c32)
$ export JWT_SECRET_BASE64=$(echo -n $JWT_SECRET | base64 -w 0)
----

* Add spec.template.spec.nodeSelector and spec.template.spec.tolerations to the capi-controller-manager of kind: Deployment.

    node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""
  tolerations:

=== DCI Physical Infrastructure Deployment `@control plane`

This section describes the procedure for deploying physical infrastructure components used in DCI.

This section shows the procedures corresponding to the area enclosed in the red frame in 2.4. Construction Flow.

image::media/media/flow6.png[width=432]

※ When deploying physical infrastructure, the secret (database-cluster-app) created during PostgreSQL setup is referenced, so this must be performed after PostgreSQL configuration.

. Generate SSH keys.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~/
$ ssh-keygen -t ed25519
* Press Enter (without input) for all prompts to complete generation.
----

.Execution Result

. Generate a secret for SSH key mounting.
+

$ kubectl create secret generic physical-infrastructure-ssh-keys --from-file=id_ed25519=$HOME/.ssh/id_ed25519

. Install DCI physical infrastructure using helm.

$ helm upgrade --install physical-infrastructure oci://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/physical-infrastructure \
   --set physical-infrastructure-cdi.image.repository=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/cdi \

   --set physical-infrastructure-maas.tls.enabled=true \
   --set physical-infrastructure-network.image.repository=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/network \
   --set physical-infrastructure-network.image.tag=${TAG_PHYSICAL} \
   --set physical-infrastructure-network.tls.enabled=true \
   --set physical-infrastructure-log.image.repository=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/log \
   --set physical-infrastructure-log.image.tag=${TAG_PHYSICAL} \
   --set physical-infrastructure-exporter.image.repository=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/exporter \
   --set physical-infrastructure-exporter.image.tag=${TAG_PHYSICAL} \
   --set global.db.api.accessUrl=https://db-api.local:8443 \
   --version v${TAG_PHYSICAL}

=== Component Deployment to Management Cluster

This section describes the procedure for deploying various components required for DCI operation to the management cluster. +
Specifically, it explains the SSH key configuration and the preparation procedures for components to be deployed to the tenant logical infrastructure.

This section shows the procedures corresponding to the area enclosed in the red frame in 2.4. Construction Flow.

image::media/media/flow7.png[width=432]

==== SSH Key Configuration `@control plane`

Register the control plane SSH keys on the CDI management server, CDI management server guest OS, network switches, and MAAS server. Note that the SSH keys generated during DCI Physical Infrastructure Deployment @control plane are used.

. Register the public key.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ ssh-copy-id -i ~/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub <user>@<target-host>  (Register public key on "CDI management server")
$ ssh-copy-id -i ~/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub <user>@<target-host>  (Register public key on "CDI management server guest OS")
$ ssh-copy-id -i ~/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub <user>@<target-host>  (Register public key on "network switch")
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
/usr/bin/ssh-copy-id: INFO: attempting to log in with the new key(s), to filter out any that are already installed
/usr/bin/ssh-copy-id: INFO: 1 key(s) remain to be installed -- if you are prompted now it is to install the new keys
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----
--

. Log in to the MAAS server GUI.

  URL:http://<MAAS IPAddr>:5240/MAAS/r
--
image::media/media/image57.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Configure SSH keys in the MAAS GUI.
+
Select "admin" at the bottom left, then select "SSH keys" in "My preferences" that appears. +
Press "import SSH key" at the top right, select "Upload" as the source, and paste and register the public key created in the pod (check the contents with cat or similar).
+
--
image::media/media/image50.png[width=1000]
--

==== Register Custom Commissioning Script to Delete Unused USB-NIC Information on MAAS Server

. Create a python script. Create it on your local PC.
+
The file name should be 49-remove-usb-nic (without extension).
+
In EXCLUDED_MAC (the list of items excluded from deletion), enter the MAC addresses of USB-NICs that are in use. If there are no USB-NICs in use, set EXCLUDED_MAC = {}.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ vi 49-remove-usb-nic
----

[source,bash]
----
#!/usr/bin/env python3
import json
import os
import pprint

EXCLUDED_MAC = {
    '00:24:9b:8a:15:d1',
    '00:24:9b:8a:04:a8',
}

def main():
    maas_resource_file = os.environ["MAAS_RESOURCES_FILE"]
    with open(maas_resource_file, 'r') as resources_file:
        data=json.load(resources_file)
    resources_file.close()
    filtered_cards = []
    usb_ports = []
    for card in data["resources"]["network"]["cards"]:
        
        if card['driver'] != 'rndis_host':
            filtered_cards.append(card)
        else:
            if card['ports'][0]['address'] in EXCLUDED_MAC:
                print(f"MAC {card['ports'][0]['address']} is in the excluded list, skipping it")
                filtered_cards.append(card)
                continue
            usb_ports.append(card['ports'][0]['id'])
            print(f"rndis_host found in resources/networks/cards/port, removing it")
    data["resources"]["network"]["cards"]=filtered_cards

    for usb_port in usb_ports:
        if usb_port in data["networks"].keys():
            del(data["networks"][usb_port])
            print(f"Removing interface {usb_port} from the networks elements")
        else:
            print(f"{usb_port} not found, skipping...")

    data_json = json.dumps(data, indent=4)
    with open(maas_resource_file, 'w') as resources_file:
        resources_file.write(data_json)
    resources_file.close()
    pprint.pprint(data["networks"])
    pprint.pprint(data["resources"]["network"]["cards"])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----
--

. Register the created script to the MAAS server.
+
Upload 49-remove-usb-nic from Settings → Commissioning scripts → "Upload commissioning script".
+
--
image::media/media/image77.jpg[width=1000]
--
+
Once the upload is complete, 49-remove-usb-nic and its contents will be displayed as shown below.
+
If you want to change the EXCLUDED_MAC settings, first delete 49-remove-usb-nic from the Commissioning scripts screen, then re-upload the modified 49-remove-usb-nic.
+
--
image::media/media/image78.jpg[width=1000]
--
