. Edit nfs-subdir-external-provisioner.yaml.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ vi nfs-subdir-external-provisioner.yaml
* Edit the NFS server IP address and export directory path below.
----

[source,bash]
----
apiVersion: addons.cluster.x-k8s.io/v1alpha1
kind: HelmChartProxy
metadata:
  name: nfs-subdir-external-provisioner 
spec:
  clusterSelector:
    matchLabels:
      dciSystem: enabled
  repoURL: https://kubernetes-sigs.github.io/nfs-subdir-external-provisioner/
  chartName: nfs-subdir-external-provisioner
  releaseName: nfs-provisioner
  version: 4.0.18
  namespace: nfs-subdir-external-provisioner 
  options:
    install:
      createNamespace: true
  valuesTemplate: |
    nfs:
      server: <NFS server IP address>  # NFS Server Address
      path: <NFS server export directory path>
    storageClass:
      name: "nfs-client"
      # defaultClass: false
      # allowVolumeExpansion: true
      # reclaimPolicy: "Delete"  # or "Retain"
      # archiveOnDelete: true
----

=== DCI Management Cluster System Normal Operation Verification `@control plane`

This section describes the procedure for verifying that the constructed DCI management cluster is operating normally.

This section shows the procedures corresponding to the area enclosed in the red frame in 2.4. Construction Flow.

image::media/media/flow8.png[width=432]

Verify that all Nodes and pods are running normally (Running) through the procedures constructed so far. +
As a normal operation verification, use an ansible script to confirm that all Nodes and pods are running normally. +
By using this script, if even one Node or pod is not Running, the result will be NG. +
Also, as a log of execution results, the following summary is displayed in ./ansible_log/result.log.

　- Whether the Node status is Ready +
　- Pod name, pod image (version), status, reason (if status is Not Running), namespace, deployed Node

. Install ansible.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo dnf -y install ansible-core
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(omitted)
Installed:
  ansible-core-1:2.14.18-1.el9.x86_64      python3-cffi-1.14.5-5.el9.x86_64          python3-cryptography-36.0.1-5.el9_6.x86_64
  python3-packaging-20.9-5.el9.noarch      python3-ply-3.11-14.el9.noarch            python3-pycparser-2.20-6.el9.noarch
  python3-pyparsing-2.4.7-9.el9.noarch     python3-resolvelib-0.5.4-5.el9.noarch     sshpass-1.09-4.el9.x86_64

Complete!
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
sudo dnf list ansible-core
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Installed Packages
ansible-core.x86_64                                 1:2.14.18-1.el9                                 @rhel-9-for-x86_64-appstream-rpms
----
--

. Create the ansible script.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ vi node_pod_summary.yaml
* Copy and paste the following to create the script.
----

[source,bash]
----
---
- name: Kubernetes Node & Pod Summary
  hosts: localhost
  connection: local
  gather_facts: false

  vars:
    log_base_path: "./ansible_log"

  tasks:
    ######################################################################
    # Node Summary
    ######################################################################
    - name: Get nodes summary
      ansible.builtin.command: kubectl get nodes --no-headers
      register: nodes_raw
      changed_when: false

    - name: Build node summary
      ansible.builtin.set_fact:
        node_summary: >-
          {{
            node_summary | default([])
            + [
                {
                  'name': item.split()[0],
                  'status': item.split()[1]
                }
              ]
          }}
      loop: "{{ nodes_raw.stdout_lines }}"

    ######################################################################
    # Pod Summary
    ######################################################################
    - name: Get all pods in JSON format
      ansible.builtin.command: kubectl get pods -A -o json
      register: pods_raw
      changed_when: false

    - name: Build pod summary
      ansible.builtin.set_fact:
        pod_summary: "{{ pod_summary | default([]) + [pod_item] }}"
      vars:
        pod: "{{ item }}"
        phase: "{{ pod.status.phase | default('Unknown') }}"

        # container waiting reason (CrashLoopBackOff, ImagePullBackOff, etc.)
        waiting_reason: >-
          {{
            (
              pod.status.containerStatuses | default([])
              | map(attribute='state.waiting')
              | select('defined')
              | map(attribute='reason')
              | select('defined')
              | list
              | first
            ) | default('')
          }}

        # pod-level reason (Pending, Evicted, etc.)
        pod_reason: "{{ pod.status.reason | default('') }}"

        # final reason
        reason: >-
          {{
            waiting_reason
            if waiting_reason != ''
            else (
              pod_reason
              if pod_reason != ''
              else phase
            )
          }}

        # Treat anything other than Running as Failed
        status: "{{ 'Running' if phase == 'Running' else 'Failed' }}"

        # If NodeName is missing, mark as NotScheduled
        node: "{{ pod.spec.nodeName | default('NotScheduled') }}"

        pod_item:
          pod: "{{ pod.metadata.name }}"
          image: "{{ pod.spec.containers[0].image }}"
          status: "{{ status }}"
          reason: "{{ reason }}"
          namespace: "{{ pod.metadata.namespace }}"
          node: "{{ node }}"

      loop: "{{ (pods_raw.stdout | from_json)['items'] }}"

    ######################################################################
    # Create log directory
    ######################################################################
    - name: Ensure log directory exists
      ansible.builtin.file:
        path: "{{ log_base_path }}"
        state: directory
        mode: '0755'

    ######################################################################
    # Write result.log
    ######################################################################
    - name: Write summary to log file
      ansible.builtin.copy:
        dest: "{{ log_base_path }}/result.log"
        content: |
          ==== Node Summary ====
          {% for n in node_summary %}
          {{ n.name }}: {{ n.status }}
          {% endfor %}

          ==== Pod Summary ====
          {% for p in pod_summary %}
          pod: {{ p.pod }}
          image: {{ p.image }}
          status: {{ p.status }}
          reason: {{ p.reason }}
          namespace: {{ p.namespace }}
          node: {{ p.node }}

          {% endfor %}
      changed_when: false

    ######################################################################
    # Fail if any pod is Failed
    ######################################################################
    - name: Fail if any pod is Failed
      ansible.builtin.fail:
        msg: "One or more pods are not Running. See {{ log_base_path }}/result.log"
      when: >
        (
          pod_summary
          | selectattr('status', 'equalto', 'Failed')
          | list
          | length
        ) > 0
----

.Execution Result

----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----

----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----

----

== Initial Configuration Procedure for Resources Used in DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure Construction (Configuration Procedure for Resource Scaling)

This chapter describes the initial configuration procedures and registration procedures for various resources required for DCI tenant logical infrastructure construction and resource scaling.

When scaling resources, please perform only the procedures applicable to the target resource. When scaling resources other than servers (cards, network equipment), only perform <<構成管理DBへのリソース情報の登録,Resource Information Registration to Configuration Management DB>>.

Also, when using network equipment with a new NOS, please also refer to <<新規NOSを搭載したNW機器への対応手順,Procedure for Supporting network Equipment with New NOS>> described in the appendix.

=== COTS Server / Compute Server BIOS Configuration

This section describes the BIOS-level initial configuration procedures to be performed before construction for COTS servers and compute servers used in DCI tenant logical infrastructure construction. +
Specifically, basic settings required for automated construction and resource management are configured, such as enabling the Redfish API, enabling SR-IOV, and PXE boot configuration.

This section shows the procedures corresponding to the area enclosed in the red frame in 2.4. Construction Flow.

image::media/media/flow41.png[width=432]

==== IPMI over LAN Enablement

This procedure enables IPMI over LAN on the target server to ensure the proper operation of DCI-2.0's power information acquisition function.
IPMI over LAN is a function for remotely managing server power and status over the network.
DCI-2.0 uses this function to check the status of Nodes.

The following shows the configuration procedure using a Dell PowerEdge R730 server as an example.

. Log in to the Integrated Dell Remote Access Controller (iDRAC). * +
* iDRAC: A dedicated management controller that comes standard with Dell servers for remote management.
+
--
Enter the username and password and click Submit.

image::media/media/image55.jpg[width=1000]
--
. Enable IPMI over LAN.
+
--
After logging in, navigate to "Overview ➡ iDRAC Settings ➡ Network" and check the box to enable IPMI over LAN.

image::media/media/image56.jpg[width=1000]
--

==== Redfish API Enablement
* This procedure should only be performed for servers that support Redfish API (i.e., when the corresponding configuration item exists).

This procedure enables Redfish API on the target server so that the DCI-2.0 Node liveness monitoring function operates correctly.
Redfish API is a function for monitoring server status (startup, shutdown, responsiveness, etc.) from outside.
DCI-2.0 uses this function to check Node status.

. Log in to the Integrated Dell Remote Access Controller (iDRAC). 

. Enable Redfish.
+
--
After logging in, navigate to "Overview ➡ iDRAC Settings ➡ Network ➡ Services" and check the box to enable Redfish.

image::media/media/image2.png[width=1000]
--

==== SR-IOV Enablement

DCI-2.0 uses SR-IOV (Single Root I/O Virtualization) to share a single physical NIC among multiple Pods. +
This procedure enables SR-IOV in the BIOS settings.

. Restart the server first to enter the BIOS screen.
+
--
Connect to iDRAC and launch the virtual console.

image::media/media/image3.png[width=1000]

In the virtual console, select "Console Controls ➡ Apply Ctrl-Alt-Del" to restart the server.

image::media/media/image6.png[width=1000]
--
. Enter the BIOS screen during server startup and enable SR-IOV.
+
--
After server restart, when the startup screen (pre-OS screen) displaying the manufacturer logo appears, press F2 to enter the BIOS screen (System Setup). +
Then, in the BIOS screen, navigate to "System Setup ➡ System BIOS ➡ Integrated Devices" and change "SR-IOV Global Enable" to Enabled.

image::media/media/image2-1.png[width=1000]
--

==== PXE Boot Configuration

For COTS servers and CDI compute servers, in order to perform PXE boot from MAAS, the NIC connected to the manager network must be set to the highest Boot priority in the server's BIOS settings.

. In the BIOS Boot settings screen, change the manager network NIC to be the first device to boot.
+
--
image::media/media/image30.png[width=1000]
--

This completes the "COTS Server / Compute Server BIOS Configuration".

=== Automated Script-Based Construction

The procedures shown in Sections 3.3 through 3.7 can also be performed using automation scripts. +
Below are the automation script names and automation overview. +
For usage instructions and details of the automation scripts, please refer to the "Automation Script Manual".

[cols="1,3", options="header"]
|===
|Script Name |Automation Overview

|manager_cluster_setup.sh
| 3.3 DCI Management Cluster Construction +
  3.4 Pre-Deployment Preparation for DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure

|manager_software_deploy.sh
| 3.5 DCI Manager Deployment +
  3.6 DCI Physical Infrastructure Deployment +
  3.7 Component Deployment to DCI Management Cluster 
|===

=== DCI Management Cluster Construction Procedure in Virtual Machine (VM) Environment
This chapter describes the procedure for constructing the DCI management cluster in a virtual machine (VM) environment.

.Prerequisites
* Must have a KVM guest image file. Example: rhel-9.6-x86++_++64-kvm.qcow2

==== VM Host Side Configuration
===== (1) VM Environment Installation

. Install QEMU/KVM, libvirt, and virt-install to build the virtualization environment.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo dnf install qemu-kvm libvirt virt-install
----

===== (2) Bridge Preparation for VM Connection

The following shows an example pattern of reassigning the IP information that was assigned to the physical interface eno1 for the manager network to br0.

. Delete the connection profile named eno1 using the network configuration tool nmcli. Note that if you are connected via SSH to this interface address, the connection will be lost. +
  (Configuration via iDRAC or similar is recommended.)
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection delete eno1
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Connection eno1 (9c92fad9-6ecb-3e6c-eb4d-8a47c6f50c04) successfully deleted.
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ nmcli connection show
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(Verify that eno1 does not exist)
----
--

. Create a bridge connection named br0 using nmcli and configure it as a logical interface.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection add type bridge con-name br0 ifname br0
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Connection 'br0' (a2e9dcf9-7272-4877-b2de-2ab147668605) successfully added.
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ nmcli connection show
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(Verify that br0 exists)
----
--

. Disable the Spanning Tree Protocol (STP) for the bridge connection br0.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection modify br0 bridge.stp no
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ nmcli -f bridge.stp connection show br0
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
bridge.stp:                             no
----
--

. Manually configure a static IP address, gateway, and DNS for bridge br0. +
  This effectively reassigns the settings that were originally assigned to eno1.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection modify br0 ipv4.method manual ipv4.addresses \
  172.18.193.16/24 ipv4.gateway 172.18.193.251 ipv4.dns 8.8.8.8,8.8.4.4
($ sudo nmcli connection modify br0 ipv4.method manual ipv4.addresses \
   <MGR-IPAddr-VMHost> ipv4.gateway <MGR-GW-VMHost> ipv4.dns <DNS-SV-IPAddr>)
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ nmcli -f ipv4.method,ipv4.addresses,ipv4.gateway,ipv4.dns connection show br0
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
ipv4.method:                            manual
ipv4.addresses:                         172.18.193.16/24
ipv4.gateway:                           172.18.193.251
ipv4.dns:                               8.8.8.8,8.8.4.4
----
--

. Add the physical interface eno1 as bridge port br0-port1 of the controller br0.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection add type ethernet port-type bridge con-name br0-port1 ifname eno1 controller br0
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Connection 'br0-port1' (1861e376-ea5b-414e-acd3-ccc90b859d0c) successfully added.
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ nmcli -f connection.interface-name,connection.master connection show br0-port1
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
connection.interface-name:              eno1
connection.master:                      br0
----
--

*The following steps are performed to prevent DHCP address acquisition

. Restart the NetworkManager service to apply the network configuration changes
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo systemctl restart NetworkManager
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----
--

. Stop the network connection named br0 using nmcli.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection down br0
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Connection 'br0' successfully deactivated (D-Bus active path: /org/freedesktop/NetworkManager/ActiveConnection/2)
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ ip addr
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(Verify that br0 does not exist)
----
--

. Start the network connection named br0 using nmcli.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection up br0
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Connection successfully activated (controller waiting for ports) (D-Bus active path: /org/freedesktop/NetworkManager/ActiveConnection/8)
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ ip addr
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(Verify that br0 exists)
----
--

. Restart the NetworkManager service to apply the network configuration changes.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo systemctl restart NetworkManager
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

[source,console]
----
No output

----
$ sudo cp rhel-9.6-x86_64-kvm.qcow2 /var/lib/libvirt/images/
($ sudo cp rhel-<RHEL Version>-x86_64-kvm.qcow2 /var/lib/libvirt/images/)
----

No output

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo ls /var/lib/libvirt/images/rhel-9.6-x86_64-kvm.qcow2
($ sudo ls /var/lib/libvirt/images/rhel-<RHEL Version>-x86_64-kvm.qcow2)

===== (4) VM Generation Script (create-vm.sh) Editing
Please refer to the following for the script content.

[source,bash]
----
#!/bin/bash

case $1 in
  "d1-f01-k8s-master01 ")       * VM name specified as argument = VMNAME
    VMNAME=d1-f01-k8s-master01  * VM name
    VCPUS=8                     * Number of CPUs to assign to VM
    RAMSIZE=16                  * Memory size to assign to VM (Unit: GB)
    DISKSIZE=100                * Disk size to assign to VM (Unit: GB)
    IPADDR=172.18.193.10        * VM manager Network IP address
    ;;
  "d1-f01-k8s-master02 ")       * When creating multiple VMs, edit and add similarly to the first one
    VMNAME=d1-f01-k8s-master02  ★
    VCPUS=4                     ★
    RAMSIZE=16                  ★
    DISKSIZE=100                ★
    IPADDR=172.18.193.11        ★
esac

# Directory where image files will be stored
IMAGE_DIR=/var/lib/libvirt/images
# VM image file name
IMAGE_FILE=${VMNAME}.qcow2
# Base image file name (must exists before running the script)
BASE_FILE=rhel-9.6-x86_64-kvm.qcow2

CIDATA_PATH=$IMAGE_DIR/cidata-${VMNAME}.iso
#CIDATA_PATH=$PWD/cidata-${VMNAME}.iso
IMAGE_PATH=$IMAGE_DIR/$IMAGE_FILE
BASE_PATH=$IMAGE_DIR/$BASE_FILE

echo "VMNAME ........ $VMNAME"
echo "VCPUS ......... $VCPUS"
echo "RAMSIZE ....... $RAMSIZE G"
echo "DISKSIZE ...... $DISKSIZE G"
echo "IPADDR ........ $IPADDR"
echo "CIDATA_PATH ... $CIDATA_PATH"
echo "IMAGE_PATH .... $IMAGE_PATH"
echo "BASE_PATH ..... $BASE_PATH"
echo
read -p "Is this OK? ('y' to proceed) " ANS
if [ "$ANS" != "y" -a "$ANS" != "Y" ]; then
  echo "Aborted"
  exit 1
fi

# Go to the image dir
pushd $IMAGE_DIR

# Check if the base image exists
if sudo [ ! -r "$BASE_FILE" ]; then
  echo "error: base image file $BASE_PATH not found."
  exit 1
fi

# Check if the VM image already exists
# if not, create the new one
if sudo [ ! -f "$IMAGE_FILE" ]; then
  echo "info: image file $IMAGE_PATH not found. creating new image"
  sudo qemu-img create -f qcow2 -F qcow2 -b "$BASE_PATH" "$IMAGE_FILE" ${DISKSIZE}G
  if [ $? -ne 0 ]; then
    echo "error: failed to create image"
    exit 1
  fi
fi

# Dump the image info
sudo qemu-img info "$IMAGE_FILE"

popd

# Check if the cloud-init data file exists
# if not, generate it
if [ ! -r $CIDATA_PATH ]; then
  mkdir -p $VMNAME
  cd $VMNAME

  cat > user-data <<-EOS
#cloud-config
password: <Password>            * Login password (login user is cloud-user by default)
chpasswd: {expire: False}
ssh_pwauth: True
timezone: "Asia/Tokyo"
EOS

  cat > network-config << EOS
network:
  version: 1
  config:
    - type: physical
      name: eth1
      subnets:
        - type: static
          address: $IPADDR/24
          gateway: 172.18.193.251* Default gateway IP address
EOS

  cat > meta-data <<-EOS
instance-id: $VMNAME
local-hostname: $VMNAME
EOS

  genisoimage -output "$CIDATA_PATH" -volid cidata -joliet -rock user-data meta-data network-config
  cd ..
fi

sudo virt-install \
  --name $VMNAME \
  --ram $(($RAMSIZE * 1024)) \
  --vcpus $VCPUS \
  --os-variant rhel9.0 \
  --arch x86_64 \
  --virt-type kvm \
  --boot hd \
  --disk $IMAGE_PATH,device=disk,bus=virtio,format=qcow2 \
  --disk $CIDATA_PATH,device=cdrom \
  --network network:default \
  --network bridge:br0 \
  --graphics none \
  --import
----

*Please edit the parts marked with ★ (VM name, CPU count, memory amount, disk amount, manager network IP address, password, default gateway) according to the required resources and network configuration. Please refer to the following as guidelines for CPU, memory, and disk.

* CPU: 4 (8 if possible)
* Memory: 16  *Unit: GB
* Disk: 100  *Unit: GB

===== (5) VM Generation Script (create-vm.sh) Execution

. Execute the script create-vm.sh to create a virtual machine with the specified name.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo ./create-vm.sh <VM-name>
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(omitted)
Red Hat Enterprise Linux 9.6 (Plow)
Kernel 5.14.0-570.12.1.el9_6.x86_64 on an x86_64

Activate the web console with: systemctl enable --now cockpit.socket

<VM-name> login:

===== (6) Post-VM Creation Work

create-vm.sh creates a VM with RHEL installed where the default route is set to eth0. This needs to be changed to eth1. Specifically, delete the default route and DNS pointing to the NAT side, and change them to the bridge-side interface.

CAUTION: If the following steps are not performed, direct access from outside the manager network subnet will not be possible

. Verify the current routing information.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ ip route
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
default via 192.168.122.1 dev eth0 proto dhcp src 192.168.122.176 metric 100
default via 192.168.10.2 dev eth1 proto static metric 101
192.168.10.0/24 dev eth1 proto kernel scope link src 192.168.10.127 metric 101
192.168.122.0/24 dev eth0 proto kernel scope link src 192.168.122.176 metric 100
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
(Does a default route pointing to eth0 exist?)
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(Verify that a default route pointing to eth0 exists)
----
--

. Verify the current DNS information.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ more /etc/resolv.conf
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
# Generated by NetworkManager
nameserver 192.168.122.1
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
(Is the nameserver 192.168.122.1?)
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(Verify that the nameserver is 192.168.122.1)
----
--

. Display information for all network connection profiles managed by NetworkManager and extract the "NAME" for eth0/eth1. In the subsequent steps, they are assumed to be "Wired connection 1" and "System eth1" respectively.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connect show
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
NAME                UUID                                  TYPE      DEVICE
Wired connection 1  23129d7b-a24b-3e79-b18d-b7a5908dd2ad  ethernet  eth0
System eth1         9c92fad9-6ecb-3e6c-eb4d-8a47c6f50c04  ethernet  eth1
lo                  bcaf87e0-8301-429d-87d2-b9e885c266c1  loopback  lo
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----
--

. Configure "Wired connection 1 (eth0)" IPv4 to not be used as the default route.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection modify "Wired connection 1" ipv4.never-default yes
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
Not required in this step; will be verified in a later step
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Not required in this step; will be verified in a later step
----
--

. Set the IPv4 DNS server for the "System eth1 (eth1)" connection to Google Public DNS.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection modify "System eth1" ipv4.dns "8.8.8.8,8.8.4.4"
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
Not required in this step; will be verified in a later step
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Not required in this step; will be verified in a later step
----
--

. Ignore the automatically obtained DNS for "Wired connection 1 (eth0)" and use manually configured DNS instead.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
 $ sudo nmcli connection modify "Wired connection 1" ipv4.ignore-auto-dns yes
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
Not required in this step; will be verified in a later step
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Not required in this step; will be verified in a later step
----
--

. Restart the NetworkManager service to apply the network configuration changes.

$ sudo systemctl restart NetworkManager
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

==== VM Image Directory Move and Mount

With the default partitioning (*), the "/" capacity is small and VM images can exhaust disk space, so the VM image directory needs to be moved and mounted. +
*Since it is difficult to configure partitions during OS installation, the default partitioning is used.

===== (1) Destination Directory Creation

. Set SELinux to Permissive mode (warnings only) to temporarily disable it.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo setenforce 0
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ getenforce
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Permissive
----
--

. Create a new directory named var-libvirt inside the /home directory.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo mkdir /home/var-libvirt
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
No output
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ ls /home
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
cloud-user  var-libvirt
----
--

. Rename the directory that stores libvirt data to .bak.

. Create a new directory named libvirt inside the /var/lib directory.

. Bind mount the /home/var-libvirt directory to /var/lib/libvirt.

. Add a bind mount configuration entry to /etc/fstab to make it persist after system reboot.
+
--
.Execution Procedure

$ echo '/home/var-libvirt /var/lib/libvirt none bind 0 0' | sudo tee -a /etc/fstab

=== RHEL Subscription Application (Deletion)

==== RHEL Subscription Application

After OS installation, to perform license authentication, connect the installed server to Red Hat Hybrid Cloud Console using RHC (Remote Host Configuration) and apply the subscription using an activation key. +
(For VMs, apply the subscription after VM creation.) +
RHC is a client tool that integrates subscription registration and Red Hat Insights enablement for RHEL systems.

Note that the following steps assume work is performed as the cloud-user user.

. Connect the Red Hat system to RHC using an activation key to apply the subscription.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo rhc connect --activation-key <RHC-activation-key> --organization <RHC-organization>
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Connecting <server> to Red Hat.
This might take a few seconds.

● Connected to Red Hat Subscription Management
● Connected to Red Hat Insights
● Activated the Remote Host Configuration daemon

Successfully connected to Red Hat!

Manage your connected systems: https://red.ht/connector
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo rhc status
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Connection status for <server>:

● Connected to Red Hat Subscription Management
● Connected to Red Hat Insights
● The Remote Host Configuration daemon is active

Manage your connected systems: https://red.ht/connector
----
--

==== RHEL Subscription Deletion

Note that when uninstalling the OS, execute the following command to delete the subscription.

. Disconnect the Red Hat system from RHC and remove it from management.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo rhc disconnect
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Disconnecting <server> from Red Hat.
This might take a few seconds.

● Deactivated the Remote Host Configuration daemon
● Disconnected from Red Hat Insights
● Disconnected from Red Hat Subscription Management

Manage your connected systems: https://red.ht/connector
----

.Verification Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo rhc status
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Connection status for <server>:

● Not connected to Red Hat Subscription Management
● Not connected to Red Hat Insights
● The Remote Host Configuration daemon is inactive

Manage your connected systems: https://red.ht/connector
----
--

==== RHEL for VDC License Application

To apply the RHEL for VDC license, perform the following steps. +
By installing virt-who on the server host that will be the VM host, it will be recognized as a Hypervisor license.

. Install virt-who.
+
--
.Execution Procedure
[source,console]
----
$ sudo dnf install virt-who
----

[source,console]

$ sudoedit /etc/virt-who.conf

#https_proxy=
